L1980: Note: M3 or NBI-61962 is the O-demethyl metabolite and named A-1323217 in AbbVie's studies. M6x or NBI-54048 is the N-dealkylated metabolite and named A-1298847 in AbbVie's studies.
L1981: The mean plasma NBI-56418 concentration peaked at 45 minutes postdose (approximately 350 ng/mL) and plasma NBI-56418 concentrations were below LOQ after approximately 16 to 24 hours postdose (Figure 3.3-1). Low levels of O-demethyl metabolite NBI-61962 were measured in plasma during 30 minutes to approximately 6 hours postdose; the mean C_{max} of approximately 5.8 ng/mL was observed at 1.5 hours postdose. Even lower concentrations of N-dealkylated metabolite NBI-54048 were measured in plasma during 2 to 6 hours postdose; the mean maximum concentration of NBI-54048 (2.0 ng/mL) was observed at 2 hours postdose.
L1982: Mean C_{max} values for the metabolites, NBI-61962 and NBI-54048, were approximately 1.7% and 0.6%, respectively, of the NBI-56418 mean C_{max} value. Similarly, the mean NBI-61962 and NBI-54048 AUC_{0-inf} values were approximately 2.4% and 3.3%, respectively, of the mean NBI-56418 AUC_{0-inf} value. The median T_{max} values for the metabolites NBI-61962 and NBI-54048 (1.5 and 3.0 hours, respectively) were higher than that for NBI-56418 (0.75 hours). The t_{1/2} for NBI-61962 was approximately 1.6 hours, which was shorter than that of the parent drug NBI-56418 (2.9 hours) and NBI-54048 (4.2 hours).
L1983: Metabolite Profiling:
L1984: In the plasma, unchanged [14C]NBI-56418 was the major (&gt;90%) component. All other metabolites were less than 10% of the radioactivity in the samples. Metabolites detected in this matrix included those formed by O-demethylation (NBI-61962), N-dealkylation (NBI-54048), glucuronidation, mono-oxidation, and/or lactam formation.
L1985: Urine radioactivity (which accounted for only 2.9% of the total radioactive dose) primarily consisted of unchanged [14C]NBI-56418 (approximately 90%), with minor to trace metabolites.
L1986: The radioactivity in the feces consisted of two major components, unchanged [14C]NBI-56418 and NBI-61962. On average, approximately 38% of the radioactive dose was eliminated as NBI-61962, 26% was unchanged NBI-56418, and the remainder was a combination of multiple minor metabolites formed by oxidative deamination, glucuronidation, oxidation, and/or lactam formation, and some unidentified metabolites. Totally, approximately 52% of the radioactive dose of NBI-56418 was excreted as metabolites.
L1987: Reviewer's Comments:
L1988: • In both urine and feces, approximately 38.9% of the radioactive dose was eliminated as O-demethyl metabolite (M3 or NBI-61962). In vitro drug metabolism data showed that O-demethylation is contributed from CYP 3A4 (43%), 2D6 (52%) and 2C8 (5%). NBI-61962 was named A-1323217 in AbbVie's studies. The hepatic impairment study (Study M12-662) showed that the AUC values of A-1323217 in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment and subjects with severe hepatic impairment were approximately 5.5-fold and 17-fold, respectively, of AUC values from subjects with normal hepatic function.• The formation of M6x (NBI-54048) through N-dealkylation pathway was mediated mainly by the CYP3A enzymes. M6x or NBI-54048 was named A-1298847 in AbbVie's studies. The exposure of NBI-54048 in subject with hepatic impairment was similar with that in subjects with normal hepatic function.
L1989: Safety Results:
L1990: There were no deaths, serious, or severe AEs and no subject discontinued the study due to an AE. A total of six treatment emergent AEs were experienced by three of six subjects (50%). Two AEs (nightmares) were possibly related to study drug. There were no clinically important changes for vital signs, ECG, and physical examination findings.
L1991: 3.4 Study M12-654 (Asian PK/PD)
L1992: Title: A Double-Blind, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled Study to Evaluate Pharmacokinetics, Pharmacodynamics, and Tolerability of Multiple Doses of Elagolix in Adult Healthy Premenopausal Japanese and Han Chinese Females
L1993: Objectives:
L1994: • To assess the safety, tolerability, and pharmacokinetics of multiple doses of elagolix under fasting conditions in healthy premenopausal Japanese and Han Chinese females• To assess the effect of elagolix on pharmacodynamics of hormones (E2, P, LH, and FSH) in healthy premenopausal Japanese and Han Chinese females
L1995: Study Design:
L1996: This Phase 1, double-blind, multiple-dose, single-center study was conducted according to a 2-group, randomized, parallel design. Forty enrolled healthy subjects were randomly assigned to a single regimen for 7 days as shown in Table 3.4-1. Subjects began fasting for approximately 10 hours prior to the morning dose. For the BID evening dose, subjects began fasting approximately 2 hours prior to the evening dose. Subjects began dosing within 2 days following the start of their menstrual cycle.
L1997: Table 3.4-1. Number of Subjects in Each Dosing Group (Study M12-654)
L1998: GroupFemale PopulationRegimensA: 150 mg QDB: 200 mg BIDC: 150 mg QD Matching PlaceboD: 200 mg BID Matching Placebo1Japanese (N = 20)88222Han Chinese (N = 20)8822
L1999: Blood samples for elagolix and metabolite assays were collected prior to dosing (0) and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, and 24 hours after the morning dose on Study Day 1; prior to dosing (0) and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 24, 30, 36, and 48 h after the morning dose on Study Day 7. Trough samples were collected prior to the morning dose on Study Days 3 – 6 or upon subject discontinuation.
L2000: Blood samples were obtained for assay of hormones at Screening and prior to morning dosing (0; E2, P, LH, and FSH) and at 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, and 24 hours after the morning dose (E2 and P only) on Study Day 1; prior to dose (0; E2, P, LH and FSH) and at 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 24, 30, 36, and 48 hours after the morning dose (E2 and P only) on Study Day 7; prior to the morning dose on Study Days 3 – 6 (E2, P, LH and FSH), or upon premature discontinuation. LH and FSH were collected on Study Day 2 prior to the morning dose, and 24 and 48 hours after the morning dose on Study Day 7.
L2001: PK Results:
L2002: Elagolix:
L2003: As shown in Figure 3.4-1, the PK profiles of elagolix in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects are similar on both Day 1 and Day 7. The PK parameters of elagolix in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects are comparable (Table 3.4-1). For the 150 mg QD regimen, no drug accumulation was observed in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects. In contrast, for the 200 mg BID regimen, the mean R_{ac} on Day 7 was 0.84 and 0.79 in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects, respectively. A similar decrease in elagolix exposure was observed in White and Black subjects on Day 21 compared to that on Day 1 (Study M12-790). The decrease in elagolix exposure may be explained by the auto-induction of CYP3A, OATP1B1 and/or BCRP by high dose elagolix.

L2014: Reviewer's Comments:
L2015: • The formation of A-1298847 was catalyzed mainly by CYP3A enzymes. The induction of CYP3A enzymes by elagolix may partially explain the accumulation ratio for A-1298847 was more than 1 on Day 7.
• For the 150 mg QD regimen, the AUC ratio of A-1298847 and elagolix on Day 7 was 0.031 and 0.036 in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects, respectively. For the 200 mg BID regimen, the AUC ratio of A-1298847 and elagolix on Day 7 was 0.035 and 0.039 in Japanese and Han Chinese subjects, respectively. The AUC ratio of A-1298847 and elagolix slightly increased with the dose escalation of elagolix but the increase is not expected to have a clinically meaningful impact.

L2026: Figure 3.4-4. Mean + SD Estradiol and Progesterone Concentrations Following Multiple Doses of Elagolix and Placebo
L2027: As shown in Figure 3.4-4, dose-dependent suppression of estradiol was observed in both Japanese and Han Chinese subjects. Japanese and Han Chinese subjects appeared to have similar estradiol concentration time profiles for both 150 mg QD and 200 mg BID groups.
L2028: Anovulatory concentrations of progesterone (&lt; 5 nM) were observed in all Japanese and Han Chinese subjects at elagolix doses of 150 mg QD and 200 mg BID, and remained lower than 5 nM at 24 hours after the last dose on Study Day 7. One Japanese subject (Subject (b) (6)) in placebo group had high progesterone concentrations beginning on Study Day 1 (&gt; 32 nM) and remained above 5 nM through Study Day 7 which resulted in higher average progesterone concentrations in the placebo group.

L2030: Figure 3.4-5. Mean + SD LH and FSH Concentrations Following Multiple Doses of Elagolix and Placebo

L2032: The suppression of FSH was observed in both Japanese and Han Chinese subjects treated with the 200 mg BID regimen but not observed in both populations treated with 150 mg QD regimen. L2043: Safety Results:
L2044: For the Japanese subjects, nine (9/16, 56%) subjects receiving elagolix and one (1/4, 25%) subject receiving placebo reported at least one treatment-emergent AE. For the Han Chinese subject, nine (9/16, 56%) subjects receiving elagolix and four (4/4, 100%) subjects receiving placebo reported at least one treatment-emergent AE. Three subjects prematurely discontinued from the study. Two subjects withdrew consent to participate in the study, and one subject discontinued with an adverse event of toothache that
L2045: was not related to study drug. No clinically significant abnormal ECG findings were reported for this study. No deaths, serious adverse events or pregnancies were reported during the study.
L2046: 3.5 Study M12-673 (Ovulation study)
L2047: Title: A Phase 1, Open-Label Study of the Effects of Elagolix on Ovarian Activity, Ovulation and Ovarian Reserve in Adult Premenopausal Females
L2048: Objectives:
L2049: • To determine the effects of different doses and dosing regimens of elagolix (alone or with the hormonal add-back therapy Activella [standard (Std) dose: estradiol 1.0 mg/norethindrone acetate 0.5 mg]) on ovulation, ovarian activity and ovarian reserve when administered to healthy, adult premenopausal females and to assess the effect of elagolix alone or with the standard-dose Activella (Std Activella) on selected endocrine hormone parameters.• To assess the safety and tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of each elagolix treatment regimen (alone or with the Std Activella).
L2050: Study Design:
L2051: This Phase 1 open-label, multi-center, multiple-dose study was conducted in premenopausal female subjects to evaluate the effects of different dosing regimens of elagolix (alone or with Std Activella) on ovulation, ovarian activity and ovarian reserve when administered to healthy, adult premenopausal females and to assess the effect of elagolix (alone or with Std Activella) on selected endocrine/hormone parameters. A total of 205 subjects (N = 205) were randomly assigned to seven treatment groups (Table 3.5-1). Women aged 18 to 40 years, inclusive, with a history of regular menstrual cycles (24 to 32 days long) were selected to participate in the study. Subjects self-administered the assigned doses of elagolix with approximately 8 ounces (240 mL) of water under fasting conditions. The study consisted of 3 periods: a Screening Period of up to 60 days prior to first dose, a Treatment Period of 3 cycles (Cycles 1 through 3), and a Follow-up Period up to 60 days (Figure 3.5-1). A cycle was defined as 28 days.
L2052: During the Treatment Period, blood samples were sparsely collected every other Friday or Saturday (Weeks 2 and 4, of Cycles 1, 2 and 3) during the clinic visit to measure the concentrations of elagolix and norethindrone in plasma. Blood samples for assay of concentrations of estradiol (E2), progesterone (P), LH, FSH, and hormones were obtained 3 times per week (M, W, F or T, Th, S schedule) throughout the entire study duration (Screening, Treatment, and Follow-up Periods). During the Screening and Treatment Periods, ovulation/inhibition of ovulation and ovarian activity were assessed via serial transvaginal ultrasound (TVU) evaluations of the leading follicle (development and size) and hormone levels (P and E2). Serial TVU were performed 3 times weekly.
L2053: Table 3.5-1. Treatment Groups in Study M12-673
L2054: RegimenNumber of Subjects Dosed100 mg BID38150 mg QD40100 mg QD9200 mg QD35200 mg BID41300 mg BID22300 mg BID + Activella20
L2055: Metabolites of Elagolix in Rat, Dog and Human Formed by Representative Pathways
L2056: Metabolic pathway diagram showing the transformation of Elagolix (NBI 54048) into various metabolites:
NBI 54048 (M6x; h,d,r) → NBI 61962 (M3; h,d,r) via O-demethylation
NBI 54048 (M6x; h,d,r) → ABT-620 (NBI 56418) via N-dealkylation
ABT-620 (NBI 56418) → M6 (h,d,r) via glucuronidation
ABT-620 (NBI 56418) → NBI 59589 (M15; h,d,r) via dehydration (lactam formation)
ABT-620 (NBI 56418) → M4b (h, d, r) via oxidation
Metabolic pathway diagram of Elagolix. The starting material is NBI 54048 (M6x; h,d,r). It can undergo N-dealkylation to form ABT-620 (NBI 56418). ABT-620 can be glucuronidated to form M6 (h,d,r) or undergo O-demethylation to form NBI 61962 (M3; h,d,r). NBI 61962 can undergo dehydration (lactam formation) to form NBI 59589 (M15; h,d,r). ABT-620 can also undergo oxidation to form M4b (h, d, r).
L2057: In vitro metabolism:
L2058: The liver microsomal and hepatocyte metabolite profiles of elagolix among various animal species including CD-1 mouse, Sprague- Dawley rat, rabbit, beagle dog, cynomolgus monkey and human were qualitatively similar; there were no sex differences. In human liver microsomes, there was no metabolic chiral inversion of elagolix (b) (4)
L2059: These results are consistent with the in vivo metabolism findings in mouse, rat, dog, and human.
L2060: In vitro studies showed that O-demethylation was primarily mediated by CYP3A4 and CYP2D6, with minor contributions from CYP2C8. N-dealkylation was catalyzed primarily by CYP3A4/5 and dehydration (lactam formation) was the result of non-enzymatic cyclization of the butyric acid. In addition to CYPs, UDP-glucuronosyl transferase enzymes and biliary excretion contribute to the clearance of elagolix.
L2061: In vivo metabolism: (See study #s RD101210, RD101246, and RD101247)
L2062: Study RD101210 "Metabolic profiling in SD rats following single 300 mg/kg dose of [14C]NBI-56418" was reviewed by Dr. Raheja and submitted to DARRTS on 4-30-08.
L2063: In vivo metabolite profiling was conducted in mouse, rat and dog, and humans using radiolabeled elagolix. There were no unique human metabolites; all circulating human metabolites were also observed in plasma or excreta of rats and dogs. (Mouse study did not include metabolite quantitation).

L2066: Metabolites in plasma:
L2067: Unchanged elagolix was the major drug-derived material in plasma in mouse (percent not given), rat (57% or greater), dog (94%) and human (93%). Reviewer notes that M14 and M15 are borderline major metabolites in the rat. The previous table lists them as minor.

L2070: Additional Information: Dose in mg/kg;
L2071: ✓ - present in mouse plasma (study did not include quantitation); metabolites reported as &lt;1% of radioactivity in plasma were detected by MS, but were below the limits of quantitation.
L2072: * Mono-oxy glucuronide metabolites 2a, b, c, &amp; d; ND - not detected
L2073: u, f - metabolite present in urine and feces
L2074: u, b - metabolite present in urine and bile.
L2075: § Metabolites M7, M11 and M12 were also detected in mouse plasma
L2076: M4c, M4d, M14y/z and M19 were detected in rat plasma
L2077: M4c, M14y/z and M18 were detected in dog plasma
L2078: M17 detected in dog feces after IV dosing only
L2079: Empty cells were not discussed; U - unknown
L2080: None of the metabolites in human plasma were classified as major (&gt;10% of parent). The metabolites detected in human plasma (all &lt;5% of plasma radioactivity) were identified as M3 (O-desmethyl elagolix), M4b (mono-oxy elagolix), M6 (elagolix glucuronide), M6x (N-desalkyl elagolix), M10 (O-desmethyl, N-desalkylated elagolix ketone glucuronide) and M17 (elagolix di-oxy lactam). M1 (O-desmethyl elagolix glucuronide), M2 (mono-oxy elagolix glucuronide), M9a (N-desalkyl elagolix ketone mono-oxy), M14 (O-desmethyl elagolix lactam) and M15 (elagolix lactam) were also present at trace levels in plasma (&lt;1%).
L2081: Metabolites in excreta (urine, bile, feces): See sponsor's Table 2.6.5.9b (not shown).
L2082: Elagolix was primarily excreted in unchanged form in urine, bile, and feces. In urine, unchanged elagolix comprised the majority (&gt;65%) of the drug-derived material across species. In feces, unchanged elagolix represented 43.7-59.0% of the dose in male-female rats (bile + feces), 25.8% of the dose in dogs and 26.3% of the dose in humans. The percentage of dose excreted as metabolites was 39%, 28%, and 52% in rats (male/female combined), dogs and humans, respectively.
L2083: The metabolic pathways involved in the clearance of elagolix were similar across species (including humans) with the exception of rats, where a greater proportion of the dose was excreted as unchanged elagolix.
L2084: A majority of the metabolites eliminated in feces were from the O-demethylation pathway, comprising 14.6-16.9% of the dose in male-female rats (bile + feces), 21.2% of the dose in dogs and 38.8% of the dose in humans. The primary metabolite in this subgroup was M3 (O-desmethyl elagolix) representing 7.9-10.7% of the dose in male-female rats (bile + feces), 10.8% of the dose in dogs and 37.6% of the dose in humans. All remaining human metabolites
L2085: accounted for &lt;10% of the dose in excreta. The most abundant of the minor metabolites were from the oxidation pathway, with M4b and M4c (both mono-oxidation metabolites of elagolix) accounting for 6.7 and 2.5% of the dose in human feces.
L2086: While the O-desmethyl (M3; NBI-61062) and the N-dealkyl (M6x; NBI-54048) metabolites of elagolix have the ability to bind to the GnRH receptor, their low systemic exposure suggests that they are not likely to contribute to the pharmacological effects of elagolix (Section 2.6.2).
L2087: Excretion
L2088: Study #s RD101209 (rat – intact and bile-duct cannulated, oral administration), RD101239 (mass balance rat, oral administration), RD101240 (mass balance dog, oral and iv administration) and sponsor's Table 2.6.5.13.
L2089: Excretion of elagolix was determined in rat (intact and bile-duct cannulated; M/F), dog (M/F), and human (M), using radiolabeled compound. Orally administered elagolix (parent and metabolites) was mainly cleared via biliary excretion and fecal elimination, with minimal renal clearance, in both nonclinical species and humans. No notable sex differences were noted. The reason for lower total recovery in the dog is not known.

L2094: Excretion in Milk: A radiolabeled excretion study in lactating rats has not been conducted. Two pre- and post-natal studies have been conducted (dose-range finding RD121000 and definitive RD13581) in which elagolix was measured in the plasma of lactating pups. In the dose range finding study, pup exposure was much less than maternal exposure, and in the definitive study, pup exposure was approximately equal to maternal exposure. Methodological differences between the two studies led the sponsor to weight the dose range finding study more heavily.

L2096: Pharmacokinetic Drug Interactions
L2097: CYP inhibition Study #s RD101207, RD101215, RD13535
L2098: The potential for elagolix to inhibit the major CYP enzymes in pooled human liver microsomes was evaluated. Elagolix was a weak inhibitor of CYP450 enzymes in human liver microsomes with IC_{50} values of 30 uM or greater for CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4/5. Elagolix showed weak time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4/5, CYP2C8 and CYP2C19 with K_i values all &gt;10 uM.
L2099: Consistent with the mechanistic DDI predictions, in a clinical DDI study, midazolam (sensitive CYP3A4 substrate) exposure (AUC) decreased with multiple doses of elagolix (300 mg BID).
L2100: While elagolix is a time dependent inhibitor of CYP2C19, the inability to quantify the elagolix induction of CYP2C19 precludes a calculation of AUCR (ratio of AUC w/ or w/out inhibitor).

L2110: Additional Information:
L2111: Elagolix (0-200 µM) was pre-incubated in human liver microsomes (HLM; final protein concentration 1-2 mg/mL) and NADPH-generating system. Elagolix was also tested using recombinant enzymes rCYP3A4 or rCYP3A5 (200 pmol/mL). Troleandomycin (10 µM) served as a positive control for metabolism-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4/5.
L2112: Tranylcypromine (300 µM) served as a positive control for inhibition of CYP2C19.
L2113: Phenelzine (50, 250 µM) served as the positive control for inhibition of CYP2C8.
L2114: The effect of two primary metabolites of elagolix NB1-61962 (M3) and NB1-54048 (M6x) on CYP enzyme activity was also measured in human liver microsomes (RD101215, review by Dr. Raheja filed on 6-22-2009).
L2115: In vitro CYP inhibition studies in human liver microsomes showed that NB1-61962 had little or no inhibitory effect on the major CYP450 enzymes (CYP1A2, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4/5) at the highest tested concentration of 50 µM. Pre-incubation showed a weak metabolism-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4/5 with the IC50 value shifting from 50 µM to 29 µM.
L2116: In humans given a dose of 150 mg of elagolix, the Cmax values detected for NB1-61962 were ~6 ng/mL or 10 nM, which means that metabolite concentrations will not be high enough to affect CYP activity.
L2117: NB1-54048 showed weak to moderate inhibition of CYP2C9, CYP2C19 and CYP2D6 with IC50 values of 22 µM, 16 µM and 8 µM, respectively, but was a relatively potent inhibitor of
L2118: CYP3A4/5, with an IC_{50} value of 1.6 \mu\text{M}. Pre-incubation of NBI-54048 in human liver microsomes showed evidence of metabolism-dependent inhibition of CYP2C9, CYP2C19 and CYP3A4/5 such that the IC_{50} values shifted from 22 \mu\text{M} to 9.6 \mu\text{M}, from 16 \mu\text{M} to 0.26 \mu\text{M} and from 1.6 to 0.43 \mu\text{M}, respectively.
L2119: Since this effect was the largest in CYP2C19 and CYP3A4/5, the sponsor carried out additional metabolism-dependent kinetic inhibition studies with NBI-54048 to determine its inactivation potential. The calculated K_i and K_{inact} values for NBI-54048 were \sim 4.6 \mu\text{M} and 0.056 \text{ min}^{-1} respectively for CYP2C19, and \sim 5.9 \mu\text{M} and 0.079 \text{ min}^{-1} for CYP3A4/5, respectively. These data indicated that NBI-54048 had a relatively high potential for inactivation of CYP2C19 and CYP3A4/5 enzymes through metabolism-dependent inhibition.
L2120: However, circulating levels of the NBI-54048 do not reach levels high enough to produce inactivation. In clinical studies where subjects were administered a 150 mg dose of elagolix the maximum concentration metabolite NBI-54048 was \sim 2.9 \text{ ng/mL} or 6 nM, representing less than 1% of the of the parent compound.
L2121: CYP induction Study #s RD13135 and RD161459
L2122: The potential for elagolix to cause CYP induction was evaluated using a reporter gene construct screening assay in human hepatocytes. Elagolix was a measurable inducer of CYP3A4, CYP2B6 and CYP2C8 mRNA; elagolix should be considered a potential inducer of CYP2C9 and CYP2C19, but the response was too low to quantify accurately, giving a variable, dose-dependent response between 1.1 and 2.4-fold over control for both enzymes (RD13135). Elagolix was not an inducer of CYP1A2.

L2130: The sponsor concluded that “Mechanistic DDI predictions based on the clinical dose and exposure indicate that elagolix has the potential to decrease the exposure of CYP3A4 and
L2131: CYP2B6 sensitive substrates as a net effect of time dependent inhibition and induction, and have minimal impact on CYP2C8 sensitive substrates. Clinical data are shown on the following page at the end of this section.
L2132: Drug transporters: Study #s RD160741 and RD161157
L2133: Review note: In vitro studies investigating the effect of elagolix on hepatic and renal transporters were conducted between 2010 and 2016. Some of these studies were submitted to IND 64802 SDN180 on 10/27/11 and reviewed by Dr. Raheja (review filed 2-16-2012). For the NDA, data from these studies were included with additional newer studies under one study report - RD160741. Additional summaries and an assessment of the likelihood of DDI in humans are presented in RD161157. Because some of the experiments were done more than once using different methodologies (for a complete list see Appendix A p 35 of RD 160741) results are summarized below primarily in tabular form.
L2134: Drug efflux transporters: P-gp, BCRP, MRP2, BSEP
L2135: Elagolix is a substrate for P-gp but not for BCRP. It is an inhibitor of BCRP, MRP2, and BSEP.

L2160: Analytical methods
L2161: Sponsor's text section 2.6.4.2.
L2162: Specific and sensitive bioanalytical assays have been developed and validated for the quantitative determination of elagolix, NBI-61962 (O-desmethyl metabolite M3) and NBI-54048 (N-dealkyl metabolite M6x) in mouse, rat, rabbit, dog, and monkey plasma. Elagolix, NBI-61962 and NBI-54048 were stable for at least 6 months in rabbit, dog and monkey plasma, and for at least 341 days in mouse plasma when stored at -70°C or below. Stability of elagolix, NBI-61962 and NBI-54048 was demonstrated for at least 355 days in rat plasma when stored at -70°C.
L2163: Across all species, the method for quantitation of elagolix in plasma was generally validated over the 5-2000 ng/mL concentration range, with a 5 ng/mL (8 nM) limit of quantitation. The methods for the quantitation of NBI-61962 were generally validated over a 2-800 ng/mL concentration range, with a 2 ng/mL limit of quantitation. The methods for quantitation of NBI-54048 were generally validated over a 1-250 ng/mL concentration range, with a 1 ng/mL limit of quantitation.

L2168: 5.2 Toxicokinetics
L2169: Over the long course of development of elagolix, a large number of TK studies were done across the various nonclinical species. For ease of review, the sponsor collated the major results from each species and presented most of them graphically (See RD150352). Briefly, this section summarizes studies that addressed the following aspects of dosing on TK parameters: formulation, gender, dose proportionality, and accumulation with repeat dosing. Human TK is given at the end of this section for comparison.
L2170: Formulation
L2171: Studies were conducted to evaluate the contribution of salt form and formulation (various aqueous vehicles, diet, or tablet) on the TK profile. Because the aqueous formulation was associated with mortality in the rodent – presumably due to aspiration and subsequent respiratory toxicity – the sponsor had to develop different formulations in order to successfully complete the long-duration tox studies. Selected results from these studies are summarized below.
L2172: Salts
L2173: Various salt forms of elagolix (b) (4) were assessed for TK in mouse, rat, and dog. There were minimal differences in exposure between the salt forms. Given the similarities in exposures between salts, the sodium salt was utilized for most of the long term definitive toxicology studies.
L2174: Formulation
L2175: Various aqueous vehicles tested in mouse and rat will not be detailed here other than to note that exposures were comparable to each other and equal to or greater than exposures when corn oil was used as vehicle. More important were the studies characterizing dietary administration in the rat and mouse, and tablet formulation in the dog, since those are the formulations used in the long duration studies.
L2176: Gavage vs diet: AUCs from gavage dosing were ~2-3-fold higher than from administration in the diet in the rat and mouse. No gender differences were noted.

L2185: 2.6.4.3. Figure 2 p 9
L2186: Gender
L2187: There were no consistent sex differences in AUCs from dogs or monkeys. AUCs in female rats were higher than in male rats when elagolix was dosed by gavage, but not when administered in the diet. AUCs in female mice were higher than in male mice only at high doses (&gt;500 mg/kg/d) when administered via diet.
L2188: Dose proportionality
L2189: Elagolix exposures were generally slightly greater than proportional to the administered doses in all species. A representative graph is presented below from mouse study #RD150352. The graph depicts dosing using various salt forms of elagolix.

Scatter plot showing Area Under the Curve (ug.hr/mL) versus Dose (mg active/kg/day) for various salt forms of elagolix in CD-1 mice. The data points show a linear relationship on the semi-logarithmic scale, indicating dose proportionality.
L2192: Accumulation with repeat dosing
L2193: Mouse: Exposures after repeated daily dosing were comparable to those obtained at the beginning of the study in CD-1 mice.
L2194: Rat: In SD rats, the AUC after repeat dosing tended to remain unchanged at low doses (&lt; 300 mg/kg), increased up to 3.5-fold after high doses (300 to 600 mg/kg) and decreased up to 68% after very high doses (1,200 mg/kg or above).
L2195: Rabbit: Exposures were 3-11-fold higher after multiple dosing in NZ white rabbits.
L2196: Dog: In the dog, the AUCs were comparable following up to 39 weeks of daily dosing up to 150 mg/kg; however, some exceptions were noted, particularly at high doses (≥ 150 mg/kg), where as much as a 5.4-fold increase in AUC was observed in the 6-week and 26-week studies.

Bar chart showing Area Under the Curve (AUC) in ug.hr/mL for Elagolix in Beagle Dogs at various doses (10, 30, 50, 75, 150, 300 mg/kg/day). The chart compares AUC on Day 1 (D1) with AUC at the end of the study (D42, D182, D273). L2199: Monkey: In contrast, AUCs were nearly 10-fold lower after multiple dosing in cynomolgus monkeys. This decline occurred early in the study, with AUCs on Day 42 comparable to those measured on Day 91 in the 13-week study. The sponsor stated that the observed decrease in AUCs in monkey with multiple dosing did not appear to be caused by liver CYP enzyme induction, but did not provide data for an explanation. Because of the marked decline in AUC following multiple dosing in the monkey, it was not selected as the non-rodent species for toxicology studies.
L2200: HUMAN PK
L2201: Sponsor's text from section 2.5.1.4.: "Elagolix is rapidly absorbed from the gastrointestinal tract, with Cmax occurring at approximately 1 hour. Elagolix affects the ovarian sex hormone cascade within hours of the first dose and has an elimination half-life of 4-6 hours. The shorter half-life as compared to other GnRH agonists is relevant, especially for patients experiencing adverse events, since the drug is eliminated within a week. The pharmacodynamic effect of elagolix on LH and FSH persists longer than the pharmacokinetic half-life, enabling efficacy with either QD or BID dosing.

L2204: a. Tmax reported as median (range).
L2205: b. N = 6 on Study Day 21 for each of the dose groups.
L2206: c. Presented as the harmonic mean, pseudo-standard deviation.
L2207: d. Ctrough is C12 for BID dose groups and C24 for the QD dose group.
L2208: e. Accumulation ratio calculated as the ratio of Study Day 21 AUCτ to Study Day 1 AUCτ.
L2209: Note: AUCτ where τ is the dosing interval (i.e., 12 hours for BID and 24 hours for QD).
L2210: On the basis of steady-state pharmacokinetic data obtained on Study Day 21, elagolix is dose proportional from 100 mg BID to 400 mg BID, with mean Tmax of 1 to 1.3 hours. After reaching Cmax, elagolix concentrations decline in a biphasic fashion, with a t1/2 of ~4 to 6 hours. Through 400 mg BID, multiple doses of elagolix result in minimal accumulation as the mean accumulation ratio (Rac) values based on AUC (Day 21/Day1) were close to 1 in all dose groups.
L2211: For calculation of MOEs relative to nonclinical dosing, the Cmax and AUC for the 200 mg BID dose on d21 will be used. Cmax = 774 ng/mL. The AUC value is doubled from 1725 ng·hr/mL to 3450 ng·hr/mL to account for exposure over 24 hrs.

L2217: 6 General Toxicology
L2218: 6.1 Single-Dose Toxicity
L2219: Non-GLP single-dose toxicity studies were conducted in rat and monkey and were not reviewed in detail. Methods and conclusions are briefly summarized below. Monkey was found to be the more sensitive species for single-dose exposure, likely due to much higher exposures for a given dose. Roughly 10-fold higher exposures were achieved in monkeys vs rats for a given dose.
L2220: Rat: Study #RD101041
L2221: Dosing: 300, 600, 1200, or 2000 mg/kg elagolix Na salt in water, oral gavage, 10 mL/kg
L2222: N = 3/sex/group SD rats
L2223: MTD was based on mortality, body weight, food consumption, clinical observations, and clinical pathology observed on Day 2. Gross pathology and organ weights were collected at necropsy on Day 4. Histopathology was not done.
L2224: 3 animals were found dead (1 F at each of the 3 highest doses). Mortality was attributed to hypovolemia due to blood re-draws. At the two highest doses, there was an increase in bilirubin (~130% of control) in females. At the high dose, there were elevated liver enzymes (less than 2X) that were within the normal range for that facility. There was no other effect on clinical and gross pathology or organ weights. Males showed 50% decreased food consumption at the high dose.
L2225: MTD was set at 2000 mg/kg. NOAEL = 300 mg/kg. At this dose, the Cmax and AUC0-24 values were 2.5 µg/mL and 22.8 µg·hr/mL, respectively. Metabolite NBI-61962 was measured.
L2226: Monkey: RD101072
L2227: Dosing: 300, 600, 1200, or 2000 mg/kg elagolix Na salt in water, oral gavage, 10 mL/kg
L2228: N = 2/sex/group cynomolgus monkey
L2229: Due to error in body weight calculations, the actual doses ranged from 1380-1452 mg/kg for the 1200 mg/kg dose group and from 2320-2400 mg/kg for the 2000 mg/kg dose group.
L2230: MTD was based on mortality, body weight, food consumption, clinical observations, and clinical pathology recorded on Day 2. Gross pathology and organ weights were recorded on Day 4 at necropsy. Liver was examined microscopically.
L2231: There was no mortality. Decreased food consumption was noted at the higher doses.
L2232: Elevation in liver enzymes (ALT, AST) and total bilirubin was observed at all doses. ALT ↑ 1.2X - 2.2X. AST (males only) ↑5.8X at 1200 mg/kg and 11.9X at 2000 mg/kg. Increase in total bilirubin was maximal (7.6-fold) at 1200 mg/kg. Kidney-associated clinical chemistry changes included increases in creatinine level of up to 2.8-fold (maximal) and BUN level of up to 1.9-fold (maximal) at 1200 mg/kg only.
L2233: No changes were observed in gross pathology or organ weights. Hepatocellular centrilobular vacuolation consistent with microvesicular fatty changes was noted only in females at doses ≥ 600 mg/kg.
L2234: The 300 mg/kg dose was considered the MTD and the NOAEL, since changes in clinical chemistry parameters including ALT, AST, total bilirubin, blood urea nitrogen, creatinine, and glucose were ≤ 2.5-fold.
L2235: Cmax and AUC0-24 at the NOAEL (using the sex with the lower mean values) was 10.2 µg/mL and 75.8 µg·hr/mL, respectively. Metabolite NBI-61962 was measured.
L2236: 6.2 Repeat-Dose Toxicity
L2237: RAT 2-week oral Reviewed by Dr. Raheja 1/12/07
L2238: A 14-day oral toxicity study of NBI-56418 in rats with a 7-day recovery. Study # RD101086 (b) (4) 882-013). GLP. N = 10 SD rats/sex/group in the main study and 3/sex/group in the control and high dose recovery groups. The dose levels were 0, 300, 600 and 1200 mg/kg/day by gavage.
L2239: Results:
L2240: Mortality: One female at 600 mg/kg/day and 5 males and 6 females in the 1200 mg/kg/day group. Gross necropsy findings consisted of discoloration of the lungs. Histopathological findings consisted of ulcerative tracheitis and/or bronchitis considered due to aspiration of test material. Cause of death in most of the animals was deemed due to gavage errors or test article in the chest cavity.
L2241: Clinical observations: In the surviving rats at the high dose there was red material around the mouth and/or nose, salivation, discolored hair, skin cold to touch, audible difficult and slow breathing, hunched posture and/or hypoactivity. Salivation at 600 and 1200 mg/kg. No clinical observations in the high dose recovery group.
L2242: Body weight: Body weight was decreased in the high dose group by 16% in males and 11% in females on day 14 and was correlated with decreased food consumption. Food consumption recovered but body weight did not fully recover at the end of the recovery period.
L2243: Clinical pathology: At the high dose: decreased hematocrit (7 – 11%); decreased Hb (11% in females only); increased reticulocytes (absolute and percent, 100- 169%); increased platelets (35 – 47%). Reticulocytes and platelets still elevated at 7-day recovery.
L2244: Gross pathology: red discoloration of lungs
L2245: Organ weights: 600 mg/kg females and 1200 mg/kg males and females had increased liver weight, increased adrenal weight, and one female with adrenal hyperplasia; decreased thymus weight, one female with thymic atrophy.
L2246: Histopathology: At the high dose, lymphoid necrosis in thymus, mandibular and mesenteric lymph nodes, and splenic atrophy in animals that died and in ¼ females at terminal sacrifice.
L2247: The sponsor considered 300 mg/kg/day as the NOAEL based on pulmonary microscopic changes, trace alveolar histiocytosis and hemorrhage at 300 mg/kg. However, these findings were not due to toxicity of the drug, but were related to the formulation.
L2248: Systemic exposure at 300 mg/kg on day 1 was as follows:
L2249: Cmax= 4.3 ug/ml and AUC0-24= 35.3 ug.h/ml in males (minimum exposure value)
L2250: Cmax= 3.3 ug/ml and AUC0-24 = 21.4 ug.h/ml in females (minimum exposure value)
L2251: RAT - 6 week oral Reviewed by Dr. Raheja 1/12/07
L2252: Toxicity study by gavage administration to SD rats for 6 weeks followed by a 4-week recovery period. NBI-56418. Study # RD101089. (b) (4) /014. GLP. Dose levels were 0, 150, 300 and 600 mg/kg/day given by gavage. There were 10/sex/group in the main study, 12/sex/group in the TK dose groups and 5/sex/group in the control and high dose recovery groups.
L2253: Results:
L2254: Mortality: One female in the low dose TK group was sacrificed in extremis. Cause of death was not described and was considered unrelated to treatment.
L2255: Clinical observations: Rales in 1/10 males and 1/12 TK females. Salivation post-dose in all treated groups.
L2256: Body weight: A slight decrease in body weight gain in males given 600 mg/day (8%) and increase in females (5%) was reported.
L2257: Clinical pathology: Increased platelet count in 300 and 600 mg/kg females (up to 17.5%) and 600 mg/kg males (18.8%). Not considered adverse / within normal range. Platelet counts returned to normal in the recovery period. Immunotox: No effect on NK cell function or numbers of T and B cells. Decreased AST and ALT and decreased triglycerides in mid and high dose males and females.
L2258: Increased urinary pH in males and females and was reversible.
L2259: Organ weight: Increased relative liver weight in high dose males and females (up to 17%); reversible.
L2260: Gross pathology and histopathology: No treatment-related findings. Lymphoid system examined according to Kuper et al., Tox Path 28(3);454. (2000).
L2261: NOAEL = 600 mg/kg/day. At this dose Cmax = 6.71 ug/ml and AUC0-24h = 82.4 ug.h/ml in males and Cmax = 3.05 ug/ml and AUC0-24h = 48.4 ug.h/ml in females
L2262: RAT 13-week oral Reviewed by Dr. Raheja 5-2-2005.
L2263: A 13-week oral toxicity study of NBI-56418 in rats. Study #RD 101077. (b) (4) 882-033. GLP. 2004. Dose levels were 0, 100, 300 and 600 mg/kg/day.
L2264: (Note: a second 13-wk oral gavage study (RD101045) comparing (b) (4) salts of elagolix and a 15 week dietary range-finding study (RD101047) were conducted in the rat but were not reviewed).
L2265: Study design:

L2267: a following 91 days of administration, 5 animals/sex were maintained for a 4-week recovery period.
L2268: b TK groups
L2269: The animals were dosed via oral gavage at a volume of 10 mL/kg for 7 days and 5 mL/kg thereafter.
L2270: Observations: Mortality, morbidity, and availability of food and water were observed twice daily. Clinical observations and body weight measurements were conducted weekly. Food consumption for the main study animals was recorded weekly. Ophthalmoscopic examination was conducted prior to dosing and then at prior to scheduled necropsy. Blood and urine samples for clinical pathology evaluation were collected from main study animals prior to scheduled necropsies. Additional blood samples for determination of testosterone and estradiol were collected pretest and from main study animals prior to scheduled necropsies. Blood samples were collected from TK animals for the determination of plasma drug concentrations on Days 1, 42 and 91.
L2271: Results:
L2272: Mortality: Five main study animals were found dead: one male treated with 300 mg/kg/day (day 18 – dosing error), two males treated with 600 mg/kg/day (days 71-dosing error and 73 - undetermined) and two females treated with 600 mg/kg/day (days 11 and 62 – both undetermined).
L2273: There were four deaths in the TK groups. One male at 300 mg/kg/day (d1 after blood collection – due to anesthesia); one male at 600 mg/kg/day (d 51 dosing error); one female at 300 mg/kg/day (d 15 dosing error); and one female at 600 mg/kg/day (d 36, undetermined).
L2274: Clinical findings: Observations included incidences of decreased activity, salivation, soft or reduced number of feces and changes in respiration (audible and difficult breathing). Since salivation was not observed after week 2 of treatment, it suggests that salivation is treatment-related, physiological mechanism of which is not explained. The difficult breathing, audible breath sounds and subsequent death of these animals was considered to be related to dosing errors and could be related to macroscopic and microscopic findings in these animals i.e. presence of edema and foreign material in the lungs.
L2275: Body weight and food consumption: No treatment related effects.
L2276: Ophthalmoscopic examination: No treatment related effects.
L2277: Urine volume increased (up to 2.2 x). Urine pH was increased in both males and females at 600 mg/kg/day. It was unclear whether these changes were test article related or incidental.
L2278: Clinical pathology: Decreased Hb and Hct in 600 mg/kg females, increased platelet counts (up to 34%) and lymphocytes (up to 29%) in both genders. Sponsor considered changes small and not treated related. Increased phosphorus, decreased ALT and cholesterol (29%) in high dose males; increased GGT (70%), total protein, globulin and cholesterol (30%) in high dose females. These changes considered not treatment-related due to lack of correlated histopathology.
L2279: As shown in the table below a significant increase was observed GGT, AST and ALT in high dose females, although none exceeded 3-fold. The mean ALT value in the high dose male group was significantly lower (p &lt; 0.05) compared to control.

L2282: 2 females in the 600 mg/kg/day group had AST values of 202 and 165 U/L and this female had ALT values of 267 and 195 U/L.
L2283: Testosterone and estradiol analysis: Testosterone levels in males prior to test article administration were between 0 and 3 ng/ml with a mean of 1.67 ng/ml. Following terminal necropsies, males treated with 0, 100, 300 and 600 mg/kg/day had mean testosterone levels of 1.2 ng/ml (range of 0- 4), 1.8 (range 0 to 4), 1.7 (range 1 to 5), and 2.2 (range 0 to 40 ng/ml), respectively. Following the recovery period, mean testosterone level in both treated and control animals were the same, 1.2 ng/ml. In females, estradiol levels prior to drug administration ranged from 0 to 12 pg/ml. Estradiol levels were very variable and overall decreased in all treated groups. Due to these variations, the sponsor stated that evaluation of the effects of NBI-56418 on estradiol levels could not be determined.
L2284: Organ weights: Significant drug-related changes were present in male and female liver and adrenal weights. Adrenal weight was increased 140% in the 600 mg/kg/day female group but was decreased 75% in the 600 mg/kg/day male group. Liver weight changes are shown below. Maximum change was 45% (liver wt relative to body wt, high dose females).

L2295: RAT 26 week oral    Reviewed by Dr. Raheja 1-12-07
L2296: Study title: A 26-week oral toxicity study in Sprague Dawley rats followed by a 4-week recovery period.

Study no.:RD101044

Study report location:Application 210450 - Sequence 0000 - RD101044 26-Week Oral Toxicity Rat Recovery

Conducting laboratory and location:(b) (4)

GLP compliance and QA statement:Yes / Yes

L2297: Key Study Findings
L2298: There was significant dose-related mortality ascribed to respiratory distress secondary to aspiration of the test article. Treatment-related clinical observations included decreased activity and respiratory distress which included rales, gasping, wheezing, salivation and shallow respiration. Histopathology findings (inflammation) in the bronchii and trachea supported the inference of aspiration of drug as a cause of respiratory distress.
L2299: Based on mortality, histopathologic changes and clinical signs of respiratory distress observed at 300 mg/kg/day, the NOAEL was set at 100 mg/kg/day. On Day 182, exposure at this dose level was Cmax and AUClast equal to 1,730 ng/ml and 7,720 ng*hr/ml, respectively in the males, and Cmax and AUClast equal to 2,050 ng/ml and 6,540 ng*hr/ml, respectively for females.
L2300: Methods

Doses:0, 50, 100, 300, 600 mg/kg

Frequency of dosing:Daily

Route of administration:Oral gavage

Dose volume:5 mL/kg

Formulation/Vehicle:Water; Formulation analysis acceptable

Species/Strain:Rat / SD

Number/Sex/Group:20/sex/group; 5/sex in the control and high dose groups for recovery

Age:8-10 wks

Weight:294-528 (M) 215-311 (F)

Satellite groups:TK 12/sex/group Sampled on days 1, 42, 91 and 182

Deviation from study protocol:
During weeks 23 to 24, due to mortality and animal welfare issues, dosing activities were halted in high dose animals, which were then sacrificed during weeks 24 to 25 as scheduled sacrifices.
Surviving high dose TK animals were reassigned to main and recovery study phases. The newly formed group 5 recovery study was allowed to recover for 29-day period. Also during week 26, 5 main study males and 4 main study females from the 300 mg/kg dose group were transferred to recovery study to provide the intermediate high dose of 300 mg/kg/day with recovery phase to assess reversibility following completion of 26-week treatment period.

L2303: Observations and Results
L2304: Mortality
L2305: There were a total of 42 deaths during the study (38 main and 4 recovery) showing dose-related trend. There were also 17 TK deaths. Mortality in different groups is shown in table below:

L2307: Clinical signs:
L2308: The major clinical signs were respiratory distress and salivation. Respiratory distress was reported in 0, 1, 0, 11 and 23 animals and salivation in 0, 0, 0, 6 and 12 animals, respectively in the 5 treatment groups. Respiratory distress included rales, gasping, wheezing, and increased/decreased/shallow respiration and was greater in severity in the 300 and 600 mg/kg dose groups. The sponsor attributed this to possible aspiration of stomach contents with dose formulation, resulting in irritation of the respiratory tract. Decreased activity was observed in 0, 1, 0, 7 and 6 animals, respectively.
L2309: There were no treatment-related clinical signs during the recovery period. The cause of death of 1/10 control and 1/10 group 5 recovery animals during the treatment phase was not determined.
L2310: Body Weights: Body weight was decreased in males given 600 mg/kg/day dose from week 5 onward. Only mild reductions were noted in high dose females.
L2311: Feed Consumption: Reduced in the high dose group during the first two weeks of treatment. Otherwise no significant changes
L2312: Ophthalmoscopy: No treatment related findings
L2313: ECG: not conducted
L2314: Hematology: No treatment-related effects
L2315: Clinical Chemistry: No treatment-related effects
L2316: Urinalysis: No treatment-related effects
L2317: Gross Pathology: Dilatation of the cecum, small intestine (duodenum, jejunum, ileum) was reported in 0, 1, 0, 3 and 17 rats, respectively, in the 5 treatment groups. Other treatment-related gross findings involved thymus (dark focus) in 1, 1, 1, 6 and 12 animals, respectively.
